> help
One action = one game-hour; queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects use the previous hour's activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.
look | signs | investigate <sign id> | actions [page] | go <place name/id> | talk <person> | <interaction> <person>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | meal | sleep | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
prices | places [page] | standing <sleep|eat|drink|warmth|lodging> <on|off> | agency <commanded|psyche>
inspect <person/item/place> | pack <item> into <container> (moves one) | unpack <item>
aid <person> | retire | depart | rations <10..150> | plan | journal (lists what you owe) | explain <event id>
Enter a menu number or stable action id. help

You arrive at Hazel Spring (4 min on foot)
1855-04-01 14:00 | FAIR | Hazel Spring
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $50.00; household $0.00. 50°F, dry.
Food 6.0 days; Medium, 33.4 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 668; Rest 961; Thirst 694; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
Cold water issues at the sandstone/dolomite contact. Neighbors leave a clean saucer; nobody admits hearing the bell below the water. Spring at Hazel Spring. The wind carries the smell of wet earth. The paths about Hazel Spring are quiet. A pale glimmer rests where the bank hides the sky. There is danger here; the ordinary rules feel uncertain. Do not follow a light across running water.
Here: no one else.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 22:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Trystan Tregear comforted Jenifer Tregear at Tregear Upper Coulee Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 12:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me. I did not know where to look, so I looked at him, which was the trouble.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-03 06:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Hazel Spring: A pale light slips between two trunks and leaves no shadow.
Since last look: no time passed.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 22:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Trystan Tregear comforted Jenifer Tregear at Tregear Upper Coulee Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 12:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me. I did not know where to look, so I looked at him, which was the trouble.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-03 06:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Hazel Spring: A pale light slips between two trunks and leaves no shadow.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 08:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 15:00 Jory Rowe comforted Richard Pascoe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-03 16:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-03 16:00 There is nothing in it. I looked at it from three sides and there is nothing in it. I shall not think of it again.
1855-04-03 16:00 Jory Rowe was in close talk with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 16:00 Richard Pascoe talked with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon.
Since last look: no time passed.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 22:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Trystan Tregear comforted Jenifer Tregear at Tregear Upper Coulee Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 12:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me. I did not know where to look, so I looked at him, which was the trouble.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-03 06:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Hazel Spring: A pale light slips between two trunks and leaves no shadow.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 08:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 15:00 Jory Rowe comforted Richard Pascoe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-03 16:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-03 16:00 There is nothing in it. I looked at it from three sides and there is nothing in it. I shall not think of it again.
1855-04-03 16:00 Jory Rowe was in close talk with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 16:00 Richard Pascoe talked with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon.
1855-04-03 22:00 Jory Rowe talked with Richard Pascoe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday late at night.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-04 00:00 I am down with want of food. Too long on salt meat and meal; the body keeps its own books.
1855-04-04 03:00 Wednesday. Peran Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.

1855-04-04 05:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern. I held my face still and counted to a hundred; it did not help.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 22:00 At the Red Oak Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 08:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 09:00 Trystan Tregear comforted Jenifer Tregear at Tregear Upper Coulee Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 12:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me. I did not know where to look, so I looked at him, which was the trouble.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss.
1855-04-03 06:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Hazel Spring: A pale light slips between two trunks and leaves no shadow.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 08:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-03 15:00 Jory Rowe comforted Richard Pascoe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-03 16:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-03 16:00 There is nothing in it. I looked at it from three sides and there is nothing in it. I shall not think of it again.
1855-04-03 16:00 Jory Rowe was in close talk with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 16:00 Richard Pascoe talked with Loveday Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon.
1855-04-03 22:00 Jory Rowe talked with Richard Pascoe at the Red Oak Tavern on Tuesday late at night.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Rain in the morning.
1855-04-04 00:00 I am down with want of food. Too long on salt meat and meal; the body keeps its own books.
1855-04-04 03:00 Wednesday. Peran Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-04 04:00 Jory Rowe talked with me at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday in the small hours.
1855-04-04 05:00 Jory Rowe slighted me at the Red Oak Tavern. I held my face still and counted to a hundred; it did not help.
1855-04-04 05:00 Loveday Rowe talked long and low with Jory Rowe at the Red Oak Tavern on Wednesday at first light.
1855-04-04 08:00 There was something to ask about at Kitchen garden and stubble (17, 18). Three tools lie with their handles pointing to the threshold.
1855-04-04 09:00 I was short with Richard Pascoe today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous. Rain in the afternoon.
1855-04-05 01:00 I was short with Loveday Rowe today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-05 15:00 Bartholomew Tregear comforted me at West sandstone face on Thursday in the afternoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-05 23:00 I went to bed parched. A person can learn a good deal about the world from the floor of an empty belly.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 05:00 Friday. Loveday Rowe comforted me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-06 07:00 Loveday Rowe helped Richard Pascoe at the Red Oak Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-07 00:00 Saturday, the 7th of April, 1855; the waning gibbous.
1855-04-07 00:00 Week 1, accounted. 17 meals, 17 cold; fresh victuals about 10 in the hundred. The board ran to hardtack (ship's biscuit), dried apples and pemmican.
1855-04-08 00:00 Sunday, the 8th of April, 1855. The Sabbath, and the last quarter.
1855-04-08 01:00 I was short with Bartholomew Tregear today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-08 09:00 I was short with Nils Dahl today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-08 17:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
Since last look: no time passed.
